Softmax gradient derivation:
Let a = softmax(y) (sum=1). Objective F=2n L(a), L=LSE(c,β). dF/dy_j = ∑_i dF/da_i * (δ_{ij}a_i - a_i a_j) = a_j (dF/da_j - ∑_i dF/da_i a_i).

Then project gradient to tangent via formula a_j(D_j - ∑ a_i D_i). More generally if S not 1, but softmax. So grad_y_j = 2n a_j (D_j - ∑_i a_i D_i). Where D_j = ∂L/∂a_j = 2h_j? Algorithm `mirror_descent(a, beta, max_iters)`:
- Normalize a sum1.
- For iter:
  c, M, score.
  p=softmax(beta(c-M)); h=corr(a,p); D=2h -? Actually gradient of L w.r.t a on affine simplex (not sum constraint) is D_i = 2h_i. If a sum fixed, constraint ∑a=1, tangent variations ∑δ=0. Gradient of L on tangent is D_i - mean?   h compute; D=2h. Maybe subtract mean to reduce drift: D_center = D - (D @ a) (since tangent projection). Multiplicative update with D_center. 
  a_new = a * np.exp(-eta * D_center) ; normalize. Score. Backtrack η.

Time budget plan:
- Start with parent. Ensure a>=0, normalize.
- Evaluate exact.
- Run a sequence of optimizers with time checks:
  1. Quick mirror descent with adaptive beta (100, 500, 2000, 10k) for few seconds each to polish.
  2. L-BFGS softmax with beta schedule for bulk.
  3. c-space sqrt projection iterations with target cap around current score and target.
  4. Coordinate descent/active peak scaling.
  5. Random/stochastic restarts from best with perturbations and quick mirror/L-BFGS.
- Track best by exact internal score and sometimes evaluate_sequence.
- Final verification and output.

Let's design exact active set optimization. The objective is piecewise quadratic. We can use "committed-saving" method:
- Maintain weights p over active peaks (top few or softmax). 
- Want solve a from KKT: h_j = constant for a_j>0. We can update a via inverse of Toeplitz? If p is a probability over c indices, matrix T_{j,l}=p_{j+l}. We need T a = λ 1 (for support). This is linear. If T invertible, a ∝ T^{-1}1. We can solve approximately? T is n×n Toeplitz with entries p_{j+l} (lower anti? It's Hankel). Could use conjugate gradient on T? Since p nonnegative, T maybe positive definite? Not sure. But we can do fixed-point: a_j = λ / h_j? If T a = λ, then a_j = λ / ∑_l T_{j,l} a_l? That's not explicit because h uses a. But multiplicative update a_j ← a_j * (λ/h_j) converges to T a = λ? This is Sinkhorn-like. Yes! If T a = λ 1, multiplicative update a_j *= λ / (T a)_j is the standard iterative scaling for equality. That's exactly our h update. Great. So choose p as weights on peaks, run matrix scaling to make T a flat. Then the convolution peaks under that p? If p is empirical of current active peaks, this is dual ascent. This may be more principled.

Algorithm `active_scaling(a, p)`:
- h=T a (cross corr). λ = geometric mean of h over support? To preserve sum after normalization, λ arbitrary. Use iterative update in log: log a_j ← log a_j + log λ - log h_j. Choose log λ = mean(log a? maybe) to reduce variance. Simpler: ratio = h / h.mean(); a /= ratio (multiplicative) then normalize. Repeat many times. This is coordinate descent in log space. It will make h flat. If p fixed, T a flat. If p is distribution over active peaks, then for those active peaks, contributions balanced. But need p update to track new peaks. This is like Fienup/LP.

We can implement scaling steps combined with p update:

This is fast and positive. We can anneal β: start low (p broad) then high (focused top peaks). η step maybe 0.1-1.

Ahligned sqrt details with rfft complex arrays length M//2+1. For k=0, sqrt positive real. For k>0, principal sqrt; alignment sign. For rfft, negative frequencies implicit. If we flip sign at positive frequency, does it preserve Hermitian? rfft only stores positive; sign flip would break Hermitian unless also flip negative, but negative is conjugate of stored? If A_new[k] sign flipped, then A_new[-k]=conj(A_new[k]) still holds for the full complex spectrum (since rfft convention). So okay. But sign choice can introduce phase jumps. Align with current A.
Implementation:
```
def _sqrt_freq_aligned(Cf, Aref):
    A = np.sqrt(Cf)
    # handle zero
    dots = np.real(np.conj(Aref) * A)
    A[dots < 0] *= -1
    # For k=0 ensure positive real if Cf[0]>0
    if Cf[0].real > 0: A[0] = np.sqrt(max(0,Cf[0].real)) - 1j*0
    return A
```
But if Cf has small imaginary due to numerical, and Aref zero? OK.

Could also use phase unwrapping: Compute phase of Cf = angle(Cf). A_phase=0.5*unwrap(phase). But Cf length symmetric; principal enough.

After a_new = irfft(A_new), its first n may have negative. Clip. But clipping changes c. Blend.

This method might be novel enough to improve.

Mirror descent implementation:
```
def _mirror_descent(a, max_iters, beta, eta_init, time_limit=None):
    a=normalize
    best=a,best_sc=score
    eta=eta_init
    for it:
       c,sc
       if improve best
       p = softmax(beta*(c-cmax))
       h = cross_corr(a,p)
       D = h  # factor 2 ignore
       D -= np.dot(a,D) # tangent
       # adaptive step: maybe normalize D: step = eta * D / (np.linalg.norm(D)+eps)
       a_new = a * np.exp(-step)
       a_new = normalize
       sc_new=score
       if sc_new < sc: eta*=1.05 else eta*=0.7
       a = a_new (or if failed, keep old and eta smaller)
       # anneal beta? external
```
Need choose step scale. D magnitude: h_i roughly ∑ a_l p ~ O(1/n^2?) If a~1/n, p~1/n, h~1/n^2. Step small. We can scale D by n? Use `D = h / (np.dot(a,h)+eps)` average, then step = eta*(D-1). Because we want multiplicative ratio. This is better: relative D = h / mean(h). Then a_new = a * relative^{-eta}. This directly implements scaling η. No norm.
```
rel = h / (h.mean()+1e-30)
# limit rel to [0.1,10] to avoid huge
a_new = a * np.exp(-eta*np.log(rel)) = a * rel^{-eta}
```
If h high, reduce. Use η 0.1-1. After normalize. This is stable. For exact scaling with p, η=1 is a full scaling step; may oscillate. Use 0.2-0.5.
We can also do multiple scaling steps per p update.

This mirror/scaling update doesn't explicitly use score derivative of max? p weights based on c. It is like coordinate. We'll use with beta increasing.

C-space projection implementation details:
```
def _c_space_optimize(a, n_iters, target_score, eta=0.5):
   a=normalize; best...
   target_M = target_score/(2*n)
   for it:
      c=conv(a); M=c.max()
      gamma = max(target_M/M, 0.9) maybe
      cap = min(M*0.95, max(target_M*1.05, M*0.8?))
      # If M already below target, cap=M*0.95 to continue.
      # Water-fill:
      c2 = c.copy()
      above = c2 > cap
      excess = c2[above]-cap; c2[above]=cap; total_excess=excess.sum()
      if total_excess > 0:
          below = ~above
          # distribute to below weighted by (cap-c2) maybe
          space = np.maximum(cap - c2, 0)
          if space.sum()>0:
              c2[below] += space[below] * (total_excess / space.sum())
          else:
              c2 += total_excess/len(c2)
      # ensure sum=1
      s=c2.sum(); if s>0 c2/=s
      # optional smooth: c2 = (1-smooth)*c2 + smooth*convolution same kernel
      A_cur = rfft(a, M)
      Cf = rfft(c2, M) # c2 length 2n-1, zero pad to M automatically
      A_new = aligned_sqrt(Cf, A_cur)
      a_new = irfft(A_new, M)[:n]
      a_new = np.maximum(a_new,0)
      # blend
      t = min(0.5, 0.05*(it+1)? maybe anneal from 0.3 to 0.1)
      a_blend = (1-t)*a + t*a_new
      a_blend=normalize
      if score improve best
      a=a_blend
   return best
```
But if water-fill pushes sum back to 1, some c may exceed cap after rescale? We cap then rescale if sum changes; if sum<1, we add to below cap so no exceed; if sum>1 (unlikely because c sum already1 and capping reduces sum), no. If we normalize after distribution, cap preserved if we only add to below. Good.

However, c2 arbitrary may not be reachable; A= sqrt FFT may produce a with much mass outside first n. We take first n and normalize; this is projection. Could be effective.

```
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

TARGET_SCORE = 1.5027

def _power2_bound(k):
    m=1
    while m < k: m <<=1
    return m

def _fft_len(n): return _power2_bound(2*n-1)

def _normalize_nonneg(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    a = np.minimum(a, 1000.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros_like(a)
    a = a/s
    return a
```

But for internal sum=1, clip 1000 irrelevant. For output, normalize to sum maybe 1.0; evaluate scale invariant. If length n large, values <1/n <1.

```
def convolution_fft(a, M=None):
    n=len(a)
    if M is None: M=_fft_len(n)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, M)[:2*n-1]
    # remove tiny negative
    c[c<0]=0? But negative due to numerical? Convolution of nonnegative should nonnegative; tiny negative can affect max. Set c = np.maximum(c,0) but sum may slight. OK.
    s=c.sum()
    if s>0: c=c/s? If a normalized sum1, sum c should1. But_rescaling c changes score if using exact? We can normalize a not c. To avoid drift, do not rescale c except maybe for weights. But tiny errors. If a sum exactly1, sum c=1 within 1e-15. We can not rescale c. For score, max(c) maybe. Use c=np.maximum(c,0).
    return c
```

If a normalized by sum, but due to FFT, sum c may be 0.999999999; score slightly off. We can normalize c to sum1 for score? Since score invariant? If we use c/c.sum(), it effectively adjusts for FFT error; okay. Use in exact score: c = convolution_fft; s=c.sum(); if s>0 c=c/s. This ensures score consistent with normalized a. It might slightly distort but fine.

```
def exact_score(a):
    a=_normalize_nonneg(a)
    n=len(a)
    if n==0 or a.sum()<0.01: return np.inf
    M=_fft_len(n)
    c=convolution_fft(a,M)
    if c.sum() <=0: return np.inf
    c=c/c.sum()
    return 2.0*n*float(c.max())
```

```
def _cross_corr(a,p,M):
    n=len(a)
    # p length 2n-1; zero pad
    PA=np.fft.rfft(p, M)
    Arev=np.fft.rfft(a[n-1::-1], M)
    h=np.fft.irfft(Arev*PA, M)[:2*n-1]
    return h[n-1:2*n-1]
```

Check dtype. If p sum1, h average ~1/n^2.

Softmax objective:
```
def _softmax_func(y, beta, n, M, reg=1e-12):
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if y.size != n:
       return 1e10, np.zeros(n)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
       y = np.nan_to_num(y, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    y = y - y.mean()
    y = y - y.max()
    e=np.exp(y)
    s=e.sum()
    if s<=0: return 1e10, np.zeros(n)
    a=e/s
    c=convolution_fft(a,M)
    cs=c.sum()
    if cs<=0: return 1e10, np.zeros(n)
    c=c/cs
    cmax=p.c.max
    scaled=beta*(c-cmax)
    scaled=np.clip(scaled,-500,500)
    ex=np.exp(scaled)
    sumex=ex.sum()
    if sumex<=0: L=cmax; weights=np.zeros_like(c); weights[np.argmax(c)]=1
    else: L=cmax+np.log(sumex)/beta; weights=ex/sumex
    h=_cross_corr(a,weights,M)
    D=2.0*h
    meanD=np.dot(a,D)
    grad_a_tangent=D-meanD
    val=2.0*n*L + reg*0.5*float(np.dot(y,y)? or mean?)
    grad=2.0*n*a*grad_a_tangent
    # add center reg: if val uses reg*0.5*mean(y)^2, grad += reg*mean(y)
    # Use val reg = reg*0.5*np.mean(y*y) maybe gradient reg*mean? Since y mean zero initially.
    m=np.mean(y)
    val += reg*0.5*m*m
    grad += reg*m
    return val, grad
```

Using reg*mean(y)^2 gradient wrt y_j = reg*mean(y), yes.

Potential issue: scipy L-BFGS expects gradient of val wrt y at original y, but we centered y internally; if m not zero, gradient includes reg*m, but the F from centered y depends on m? We compute y_c=y-m. F(y_c). dF/dy_j = dF/dy_c_j (since dy_c/dy = I - 1/n 1, but F invariant along constant, gradient orthogonal; plus reg). If we compute grad for y_c and add reg*m, okay. But val uses m^2; good.

However, inside objective, `y = y - y.mean()` modifies local copy; scipy's x remains maybe off-center. The line search will evaluate with reg pulling mean to0. Good.

We can reduce dimension by fixing y[0]=0? Not necessary.

```
def run_lbfgs_softmax(a, beta, maxiter, maxfun, M, n):
    a_safe=np.maximum(a,1e-15); a_safe/=a_safe.sum()
    y=np.log(a_safe)
    y-=y.mean()
    res=minimize(_softmax_func, y, args=(beta,n,M,1e-12), method='L-BFGS-B'? no bounds, use L-BFGS-B? L-BFGS-B can handle no bounds. Use 'L-BFGS-B' with jac=True.
    options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxfun,'ftol':1e-14,'gtol':1e-10,'maxls':40,'disp':False}
```

```
def _aligned_sqrt(Cf, Aref):
    A = np.sqrt(Cf.astype(complex))
    # ensure DC positive
    if Cf.size:
        if Cf[0].real >=0:
            A[0] = np.sqrt(max(0.0,Cf[0].real)) - 0j
    if Aref is not None and len(Aref)==len(A):
        dot = np.real(np.conj(Aref) * A)
        mask = dot < 0
        A[mask] *= -1.0
    # remove tiny imaginary at DC
    return A
```

```
def _flatten_c(c, cap):
   c=c.copy()
   s=c.sum()
   if s<=0: return c
   c=c/s

       # maybe apply slight smoothing or cap lower? return c
```

```
def _box_projection(c, cap):
   while iterations:
     capped=np.minimum(c, cap); sumc=capped.sum()
     if sumc >=1: # cap too high? We can project by lowering? Actually if all below cap sum=1, no change.

        below=c<cap
```

Implement `_lse_weights(c,beta)`:
```
def _lse_weights(c,beta):
    cmax=float(np.max(c))
    scaled=beta*(c-cmax)
    scaled=np.clip(scaled,-500,500)
    ex=np.exp(scaled)
    s=ex.sum()
    if s <= 0: 
       w=np.zeros_like(c); idx=int(np.argmax(c)); w[idx]=1; return cmax,w
    w=ex/s
    L=cmax+math.log(s)/beta
    return L,w
```

`_softmax_obj` should avoid copying large y too much? OK.

In `_softmax_obj`, for very high beta, weights maybe concentrated. If L uses c normalized. Good.

L-BFGS maxiter calculation: If no deadline, use args. Implement:
```
def run_lbfgs(a, beta, maxiter, maxfun, M, n, deadline=None):
    a=_normalize(a)
    a_safe=np.maximum(a, 1e-15)
    a_safe=a_safe/a_safe.sum()
    y=np.log(a_safe); y-=y.mean()
    reg=1e-10
    try:
       res=minimize(_softmax_func, y, args=(beta,n,M,reg), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True,
                    options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxfun,'ftol':1e-14,'gtol':1e-10,'maxls':40,'disp':False})
       x=res.x
       a_out=_softmax_from_y(x)
    except Exception:
       a_out=a_safe
    sc=exact_score(a_out)
    return a_out, sc
```
If maxfun not int? ensure.

Potential issue: `L-BFGS-B` with no bounds still uses bounds? OK. Could use method='BFGS' but L-BFGS-B fine.

Mirror/Scaling function details. Let's combine into `run_scaling` with beta annealing.

```
def run_scaling(a, M, n, eta=0.4, max_outer=5000, inner=5, beta=500, beta_inc=1.1, deadline=None):
    a=_normalize(a); best=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score(a)
    beta=float(beta); eta_cur=float(eta)
    fail_count=0
    outer=0
    while outer < max_outer:
        if deadline and time.monotonic()>deadline: break
        c=convolution_fft(a,M); c/=c.sum(); cmax=float(c.max()); sc=2*n*cmax
        if sc < best_sc: best_sc=sc; best=a.copy()
        L,w=_lse_weights(c,beta)
        # inner scaling steps
        for sub in range(inner):
            h=_cross_corr(a,w,M)
            h=np.maximum(h,1e-30)
            meanh=float(np.dot(a,h)) # a sum1
            if meanh <=0: break
            rel=h/meanh
            rel=np.clip(rel,1e-6,1e6)
            log_step=-eta_cur*np.log(rel)
            log_step=np.clip(log_step,-2.5,2.5)
            a_new=a*np.exp(log_step)
            a_new=_normalize(a_new)
            a=a_new
        outer +=1
        # every few outer recompute beta increase
        if outer % 25 ==0:
           beta *= 1.25
           eta_cur = max(0.05, eta_cur*0.98)
    return best, best_sc
```
But this doesn't check score after each inner; if score worsens, it still proceeds. Could do backtracking: after inner, compute sc; if > previous sc significantly, reduce eta and revert? We can keep prev_a. Simpler: before inner, old=a.copy(); after inner, sc_new; if sc_new < sc_prev accept, else a=old and eta*=0.7. But inner may initially worsen then improve; we can allow. Use small eta.
```
prev_a=a.copy(); prev_sc=sc
for sub: ...
sc_new=exact_score(a)
if sc_new < prev_sc:
   if sc_new < best_sc: update
   eta_cur=min(1.0, eta_cur*1.03)
else:
   a=prev_a; eta_cur*=0.75
```
This is safer but slower (score after inner). We can set inner=3-5.

C-space optimize:
```
def c_space_optimize(a, M, n, target_score, max_iters=2000, deadline=None, blend=0.25):
    a=_normalize(a); best=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score(a)
    target_M=target_score/(2*n)
    blend_cur=blend
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if deadline... 
       c=convolution_fft(a,M); c/=c.sum(); Mcur=c.max(); sc=2*n*Mcur
       update best
       if Mcur <= target_M*1.01:
           cap = Mcur*0.98
       else:
           cap = np.sqrt(Mcur * (target_M*1.05))
           if cap > Mcur: cap = Mcur*0.95 # safety
           if cap <= 0: cap = Mcur*0.95
       c2=_box_projection(c, cap)
       # optional smooth c2 light
       if n>16:
           kernel=np.exp(-0.5*(np.arange(-3,4)/2.0)**2); kernel/=kernel.sum()
           c2_full=np.zeros(2*n-1); c2_full[:]=c2
           cs=np.convolve(c2_full, kernel, mode='same')
           c2=0.9*c2_full +0.1*cs
           s=c2.sum(); if s>0 c2/=s
       A_cur=np.fft.rfft(a,M)
       Cf=np.fft.rfft(c2,M)
       A_new=_aligned_sqrt(Cf, A_cur)
       a_new=np.fft.irfft(A_new,M)[:n]
       a_new=np.maximum(a_new,0.0)
       # blend
       a_blend=(1.0 - blend_cur)*a + blend_cur*a_new
       a_blend=_normalize(a_blend)
       sc_new=exact_score(a_blend)
       if sc_new < sc:
           a=a_blend
           if sc_new < best_sc: best_sc=sc_new; best=a_blend.copy()
           blend_cur=min(0.5, blend_cur*1.02)
       else:
           blend_cur=max(0.05, blend_cur*0.9)
       # decrease blend slowly
       blend_cur=max(0.05, blend_cur*(1.0 - 0.0001*it)?)
    return best,best_sc
```
If target_M is below feasible, c-space may not improve. But cap geometric to target.

Box projection function robust:
```
def _box_projection(c, cap):
    c=c.copy()
    s=c.sum()
    if s<=0: return c
    c=c/s
    if cap <=0: return c
    for it in range(20):
        above = c > cap
        capped = np.minimum(c, cap)
        sumc = capped.sum()
        if sumc >= 1.0 - 1e-15:
            # cap not binding or too high; if no above, return c
            if not np.any(above):
                return c
            # If sumc already >=1, lowering not needed; return c? But we wanted cap. We can return c / c.sum()
            return c
        deficit = 1.0 - sumc
        below = ~above
        space = np.where(below, cap - c, 0.0)
        capsum = space.sum()
        if capsum <= 1e-30:
            # infeasible, return normalized capped
            return c / s if s>0 else c
        if deficit <= capsum * (1.0 + 1e-12):
            new = capped.copy()
            new += (space / capsum) * deficit
            # due to numerical, clip to cap and renormalize
            new = np.minimum(new, cap)
            ns = new.sum()
            if ns >0:
                new = new / ns
            return new
        else:
            cap *= 1.05
    return np.minimum(c, cap) / np.minimum(c, cap).sum()
```
But if `sumc >=1` capped, that means original had no excess or cap high; return c.

Maybe simpler: `new = np.minimum(c, cap); s=new.sum(); if s<1: add deficit to normalized space; else return c`. If capacity insufficient, scale.

We can implement simpler and not worry.

Stochastic restarts function:
```
def stochastic_optimize(a, rng, n, M, deadline, quick_budget each):
    best=a; best_sc=exact_score(a)
    attempts=0
    while time<deadline and attempts<...:
       kind=rng.integers(0,6)
       a_p=perturb(best, rng, kind)
       # quick scaling or L-BFGS
       if time<deadline-30:
          a_opt, sc = run_scaling(a_p, M,n, eta=0.3, max_outer=200, inner=3, beta=1000, deadline=deadline-30)
          if sc<best_sc: update
       else: break
       attempts++
    return best,best_sc
```

```
def _active_weights(c, beta=None, top_frac=1e-4):
   cmax=c.max()
```

Perturbations detail:
```
def perturb(a, rng, kind):
    a=a.copy(); n=len(a)
    x=np.arange(n)
    if kind==0: a *= np.exp(0.02*rng.standard_normal(n))
    kind==1: a *= np.exp(0.05*rng.standard_normal(n)); smooth sigma=3
    kind==2: freq=rng.integers(1,8); phase=rng.uniform(0,2pi); a *= 1+0.08*np.cos(2pi*freq*x/n+phase)
    kind==3: block size max(1,n//50); for 5 blocks: idx=rng.integers(0,n-bs); a[idx:idx+bs]*=np.exp(0.1*rng.normal())
    kind==4: # zero random 5%
       mask=rng.random(n) < 0.05; a[mask]=0
    kind==5: # reverse-smooth: a = gaussian filter with sigma rng 1-5
    kind==6: # add small uniform random
    a=np.maximum(a,0); normalize
    return a
```

Another projection: Instead of sqrt of flattened c, we can minimize ||c - target|| in frequency by acting on A: set A_new = A * sqrt(C_target / C_current) (amplitude scaling in A), similar to Fienup but for A^2 not |A|^2. This preserves phase of A and adjusts magnitude so A_new^2 = A^2 * C_target/C_current = C_target. This is better! Since C_current = A^2. We can choose target c_time (flattened), FFT to C_target, then A_new = A * sqrt(C_target / C_current), aligned by phase. Then inverse. This avoids branch sign flips because ratio uses current A^2 phase. If C_target and C_current nonzero, sqrt ratio. This is analogous to Fienup spectral shaping but for convolution square. Excellent.

Let's implement `_c_projection_step(a, c_target, M, n, strength)`:
```
def _c_projection_step(a, c_target, M, n, strength=0.5):
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    C = A * A
    Ct = np.fft.rfft(c_target, M)
    ratio = np.ones_like(C, dtype=np.complex128)
    mask = np.abs(C) > 1e-20
    ratio[mask] = Ct[mask] / C[mask]
    # If ratio huge or zero, clip magnitude
    mag = np.abs(ratio)
    mag = np.clip(mag, 1e-6, 1e6)
    phase = np.angle(ratio)
    newmag = mag ** (0.5*strength)
    newphase = 0.5*strength*phase
    factor = newmag * (np.cos(newphase)+1j*np.sin(newphase))
    # Ensure DC factor positive real? C[0]>0, Ct[0]>=0. ratio positive. factor positive.
    Anew = A * factor
    a_new = np.fft.irfft(Anew, M)[:n]
    return np.maximum(a_new, 0.0)
```
If C[0]=1, Ct[1]=1? c_target sum1, ratio DC=1. Good.

This projection enforces c in frequency partially. It may be better than explicit sqrt. Use strength anneal from 0.2 to 0.8. If score worsen, reduce strength.

- space = np.maximum(cap-new,0); if space.sum>0 add excess*space/space.sum else add excess/len.
- If some >cap due to capacity, renormalize? `new = np.maximum(new,0); new /= new.sum()`. This may slightly exceed cap if capacity insufficient. OK.
- Optional smoothing: convolve with small kernel then renormalize. Smoothing may help.
Could also apply a floor: set new = new + floor*uniform? no.

```
def c_space_optimize(a, M, n, target_score, max_iters=10000, strength0=0.3, strength_max=0.8, deadline=None):
    a=normalize; best...
    target_M=target_score/(2*n)
    strength=strength0
    for it:
       c=conv...; Mcur=c.max(); sc=...
       update best
       if Mcur <= target_M*1.02:
           cap = Mcur*0.985
       else:
           cap = np.sqrt(Mcur * (target_M*1.03))
           if cap >= Mcur: cap = Mcur*0.95
       c_target=_make_target_c(c, cap, smooth=True if n>64 else False)
       a_proj=_c_projection_step(a,c_target,M,n,strength)
       a_new=(1-strength)*a + strength*a_proj? Actually projection already strength in frequency; additional blend maybe 0.5.
       a_new = np.maximum((1.0-0.5*strength)*a + (0.5*strength)*a_proj,0); normalize
       sc_new=score
       if sc_new < sc:
          a=a_new; update; strength=min(max, strength*1.03)
       else:
          strength=max(0.1, strength*0.9)
    return best
```

```
a_new = (1.0 - blend)*a + blend*a_proj
blend = min(0.8, strength*0.8)
```

Pseudo:
```
def run_alternating(a, M, n, target_score, deadline, beta=500, eta=0.3, strength=0.25):
    a=norm; best...
    step=0
    while time<deadline:
       # scaling block
       c=conv...
       sc...
       update
       L,w=_lse_weights(c,min(beta,200000))
       old=a.copy(); old_sc=sc
       for sub in range(2):
           h=_cross_corr(a,w,M); update a
       sc_new=score(a)
       if sc_new<old_sc: eta*=1.02; beta*=1.1
       else: a=old; eta*=0.8; beta*=0.95? (don't decrease beta always)
       # c projection every 3 blocks
       if step % 3 == 2:
           Mcur=...; target_M=target_score/(2*n)
           cap = ...
           c_target=...
           a_proj=_c_projection_step(a,c_target,M,n,strength)
           a_new=normalize(0.5*a +0.5*a_proj)
           sc_c=score(a_new)
           if sc_c < score(a): a=a_new; strength=min(0.8, strength*1.05)
           else: strength=max(0.1, strength*0.9)
       step+=1
       # update best each time
    return best,best_sc
```

Implement `run_lbfgs_schedule(a,M,n,deadline)`:
```
betas=[500,2000,10000,50000,100000]
for beta in betas:
   if time>deadline: break

   # allocate maxiter based on beta: lower beta more, high beta fewer? maybe maxiter = int(min(20000, max(500, remaining*2)))
   maxiter = int(min(15000, max(500, remaining*3)))
   maxfun=maxiter*30
   a_sc, sc = run_lbfgs(a,beta,maxiter,maxfun,M,n,deadline=deadline)
   if sc < best: update
   # quick scaling polish with deadline now+? maybe
return best
```
But if deadline is end of phase, passing deadline to run_lbfgs not used; min no internal time check. It may run full maxfun exceeding phase. We need ensure maxiter/time. Since cannot interrupt minimize, set conservative maxiter. Estimate time per function? We can sample: run a few? Simpler: maxiter = int(min(8000, max(200, remaining*1.5))). For 2000s remaining, 8000. 8000 evaluations maybe maybe 20s for n 8192. OK. Use lower.
Maybe run multiple successive and check time.

We can define `run_lbfgs_with_stop`? scipy cannot be stopped. Use small maxfun.

Maybe skip L-BFGS if alternating good? But L-BFGS may fine-tune. Keep moderate.

Could implement a custom L-BFGS with time check? Complex. Scipy acceptable.

Maybe use `scipy.optimize.minimize` with `maxiter` but no time; if it takes too long, could exceed budget and final not returned? But we reserve. Need safe maxiter. For n=8192, one function eval maybe: conv FFT 32768 ~0.002s, cross FFT ~0.002, total 0.004, 8000=>32s. Good. For n=12000 M=32768? Actually 2n=24000, M=32768, similar. 8000=>30s. OK. Let's write code carefully.

Detailed helper implementations:

```
def _power2(k):
    return 1 << (int(k)-1).bit_length() if k>1 else 1
```
But for k=1, (0).bit_length? Use:
```
def _next_power2(m):
    p=1
    while p < m: p <<=1
    return p
```
Simple.

`M = _next_power2(2*n-1)`.

`_normalize(a)`:
```
def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.zeros_like(a, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s
```

`_softmax_from_y(y)`:
```
def _softmax_from_y(y):
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
       y = np.nan_to_num(y, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    y = y - y.max()
    e = np.exp(y)
    s = e.sum()
    if s <= 0:
       return np.ones(y.size)/y.size
    return e/s
```

`_convolution_fft(a,M)`:
```
def _convolution_fft(a, M=None):
    n = a.size
    if M is None: M = _next_power2(2*n-1)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, M)
    c = c[:2*n-1]
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    s = float(c.sum())
    if s > 0:
        c = c / s
    return c
```
Should we normalize c? As said, yes for exact score. But in gradients, if c normalized due to FFT error, D derivative assumes c from a sum1. Tiny normalization not issue. For c-space target, c normalized.

`exact_score(a)` use `_convolution_fft` (which normalizes c). It also normalizes a. But if a sum<0.01? internal no; if zeros return inf.
```
def exact_score(a):
    a=_normalize(a)
    if a.size ==0: return np.inf
    if a.sum() < 0.01? Since normalized either 0 or1. if all zero return inf.
    n=a.size; M=_next_power2(2*n-1)
    c=_convolution_fft(a,M)
    m=float(c.max())
    return 2.0*n*m
```
But if a all zero, _convolution_fft c sum0 returns zeros, score0 incorrectly. Check after normalize: if a.sum() <=0 return inf. Since normalize returns zeros if sum0; a.sum()=0. So:
```
if a.sum() <= 0: return np.inf
```

`_cross_corr(a,p,M)`:
```
def _cross_corr(a,p,M):
    n=a.size
    # p may length not 2n-1; pad
    PA=np.fft.rfft(p, M)
    Arev=np.fft.rfft(a[n-1::-1], M)
    h=np.fft.irfft(Arev * PA, M)
    h=h[:2*n-1]
    return h[n-1:2*n-1]
```
If p has length 2n-1, OK.

`_lse_weights(c,beta)`:
```
def _lse_weights(c,beta):
    cmax=float(c.max())
    if beta <=0:
       w=np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1; return cmax,w
    scaled=beta*(c-cmax)
    scaled=np.clip(scaled,-500,500)
    ex=np.exp(scaled)
    s=float(ex.sum())
    if s <=0:
       w=np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1; return cmax,w
    w=ex/s
    L=cmax + math.log(s)/beta
    return L,w
```

`_top_weights(c,k)`:
```
def _top_weights(c,k):
    k=int(min(k,c.size))
    idx=np.argsort(c)[::-1][:k]
    w=np.zeros_like(c)
    w[idx]=1.0/k
    float(c.max()), w
```
Maybe if k=0 return max.

`_softmax_obj(y, beta, n, M, reg)`:
Need ensure y length n. If scipy passes x maybe shape. Use reshape.
```
def _softmax_obj(y, beta, n, M, reg):
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if y.size != n:
       return 1e12, np.zeros(n)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
       y = np.nan_to_num(y, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    y = y - float(np.mean(y))
    a = _softmax_from_y(y)
    c = _convolution_fft(a, M)
    L,w = _lse_weights(c, beta)
    h = _cross_corr(a, w, M)
    D = 2.0 * h
    meanD = float(np.dot(a, D))
    grad_a = D - meanD
    grad = 2.0 * n * a * grad_a
    mean_y = float(np.mean(y))
    val = 2.0*n*L + 0.5*reg*mean_y*mean_y
    if reg > 0:
       grad = grad + reg*mean_y
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(val)) or not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
       val = 1e12 + val if finite? 
       grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    return float(val), grad
```
But y centered in obj; grad calculated with centered y? `mean_y` after center is 0, so reg gradient zero, not pulling original mean to zero. We need use original mean before center for reg. Let's compute:
```
y_orig = np.asarray...
m_orig = float(np.mean(y_orig))
y = y_orig - m_orig
...
val=... +0.5*reg*m_orig*m_orig
grad = ... + reg*m_orig
```
But the softmax objective invariant to original mean; the returned grad should be wrt y_orig. The non-reg part is orthogonal, adding reg*m_orig. Good. Use that.

Also, if `y` centered, `_softmax_from_y` uses y. Good.

Potential problem: Since objective non-reg invariant to constant, L-BFGS may have zero gradient along constant except reg small; reg*mean will center. Good.

`run_lbfgs`:
```
def run_lbfgs(a, beta, maxiter, maxfun, M, n):
    a = _normalize(a)
    if a.sum()<=0: return a, np.inf
    a_safe = np.maximum(a, 1e-15)
    a_safe = a_safe / a_safe.sum()
    y = np.log(a_safe)
    y = y - y.mean()
    reg=1e-10
    try:
       res = minimize(_softmax_obj, y, args=(beta, n, M, reg), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True,
                      options={'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'maxfun': int(maxfun), 'ftol':1e-14, 'gtol':1e-10, 'maxls':30, 'disp':False})
       x = res.x
       a_out = _softmax_from_y(x)
    except Exception:
       a_out = a_safe
    sc = exact_score(a_out)
    if not np.isfinite(sc): a_out=a; sc=exact_score(a)
    return a_out, sc
```
`maxfun` must int > maxiter? set.

`run_scaling`:
```
def run_scaling(a, M, n, eta=0.35, max_outer=100000, inner=2, beta=1000.0, beta_max=200000.0, top_every=0, top_k=20, deadline=None):
    a=_normalize(a)
    if a.sum()<=0: return a, np.inf
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score(a)
    eta_cur=float(eta); beta_cur=float(beta)
    outer=0
    while outer < max_outer:
        if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() > deadline: break
        c=_convolution_fft(a,M)
        cmax=float(c.max()); sc=2.0*n*cmax
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc=sc; best=a.copy()
        if top_every>0 and (outer % top_every)==top_every-1:
            w=_top_weights(c, top_k)[1]
        else:
            L,w=_lse_weights(c, beta_cur)
        old=a.copy(); old_sc=sc
        for sub in range(inner):
            h=_cross_corr(a,w,M)
            h=np.where(np.isfinite(h), h, 0.0)
            h=np.maximum(h, 1e-30)
            logh=np.log(h)
            logmean=float(np.dot(a, logh))
            logstep=-eta_cur*(logh-logmean)
            logstep=np.clip(logstep, -2.5, 2.5)
            a_new=a*np.exp(logstep)
            a_new=_normalize(a_new)
            if a_new.sum() <=0: break
            a=a_new
        sc_new=exact_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < old_sc:
            if sc_new < best_sc:
                best_sc=sc_new; best=a.copy()
            eta_cur=min(0.9, eta_cur*1.03)
        else:
            a=old
            eta_cur=max(0.02, eta_cur*0.75)
        beta_cur=min(beta_max, beta_cur*1.08)
        outer +=1
    return best, best_sc
```
If logmean nan? h positive. If a has zeros, dot a logh ignores zeros. Good.
If score not finite, revert.

`run_cspace`:
First `_make_target_c`:
```
def _make_target_c(c, cap, smooth=False):
    c=c.copy()
    s=c.sum()
    if s<=0: return c
    c=c/s
    cap=float(cap)
    if cap <=0: return c
    if cap >= float(c.max()): return c
    new = np.minimum(c, cap)
    sumnew=float(new.sum())
    deficit=1.0-sumnew
    if deficit > 0:
       space=np.maximum(cap-new,0.0)
       spsum=float(space.sum())
       if spsum > 1e-30:
          scale = deficit / spsum
          if scale > 1.0:
             # capacity insufficient: scale new to sum1
             new = new / sumnew if sumnew>0 else c
          else:
             new = new + space * scale
       else:
          new = new / sumnew if sumnew>0 else c
    new = np.maximum(new,0.0)
    ns=float(new.sum())
    if ns>0: new=new/ns
    if smooth and new.size>16:
        ks=np.arange(-3,4)
        ker=np.exp(-0.5*(ks/2.0)**2)
        ker=ker/ker.sum()
        tmp=np.convolve(new, ker, mode='same')
        new=0.8*new+0.2*tmp
        ns=tmp? new sum; normalize
        new=np.maximum(new,0); new/=new.sum()
    return new
```
If scale>1 fallback normalized capped (max may exceed cap but less).

`_c_projection_step(a,c_target,M,n,strength)`:
```
def _c_projection_step(a,c_target,M,n,strength):
    strength=float(np.clip(strength,0.01,1.0))
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,M)
    C=A*A
    Ct=np.fft.rfft(c_target,M)
    ratio=np.ones_like(C, dtype=np.complex128)
    mask=np.abs(C)>1e-20
    ratio[mask]=Ct[mask]/C[mask]
    # If Ct zero and C big, ratio zero; log issue. Use min magnitude.
    mag=np.abs(ratio)
    mag=mag*(mag>0)
    mag=np.clip(mag, 1e-8, 1e8)
    pha=np.angle(ratio)
    logfac=0.5*strength*(np.log(mag)+1j*pha)
    # To avoid large phase, wrap pha to [-pi,pi] already.
    factor=np.exp(logfac)
    # Ensure DC factor not introducing phase: if Ct[0] and C[0] positive, factor positive.
    if Ct.size>0 and C[0].real > 0 and Ct[0].real >=0:
        factor[0]=max(1e-8, (np.abs(Ct[0].real)/max(C[0].real,1e-8)) ** (0.5*strength))
    Anew=A*factor
    a_new=np.fft.irfft(Anew,M)[:n]
    a_new=np.where(np.isfinite(a_new),a_new,0.0)
    return np.maximum(a_new,0.0)
```
If ratio has zero because Ct zero, mag set 1e-8 causing factor small; maybe target zero at some freq not desirable? Ct from c_target nonnegative FFT can have zeros. OK.

`run_cspace`:
```
def run_cspace(a, M,n, target_score, max_iters=10000, strength0=0.25, strength_max=0.7, deadline=None):
    a=_normalize(a); if zero return
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score(a)
    target_M=target_score/(2*n)
    strength=strength0
    it=0
    while it<max_iters:
       if deadline and time>deadline: break
       c=_convolution_fft(a,M); Mcur=c.max(); sc=2*n*Mcur
       update
       if Mcur <= 1e-15: break
       if Mcur <= target_M*1.02:
           cap = Mcur*0.99
       else:
           cap = math.sqrt(Mcur * (target_M*1.03))
           if cap <= 0: cap = Mcur*0.5
           if cap >= Mcur: cap = Mcur*0.95
       c_target=_make_target_c(c, cap, smooth=True)
       a_proj=_c_projection_step(a,c_target,M,n,strength)
       a_proj=_normalize(a_proj)
       blend = min(0.85, 0.3 + 0.5*strength)
       a_new=(1-blend)*a + blend*a_proj
       a_new=_normalize(a_new)
       sc_new=exact_score(a_new)
       if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < sc:
          a=a_new
          update best
          strength=min(strength_max, strength*1.04)
       else:
          strength=max(0.08, strength*0.9)
       it+=1
    return best,best_sc
```
If target_M below current, cap geometric. If current already lower, cap=0.99M reduce. Good.

`run_alternating`:
```
def run_alternating(a, M,n, target_score, deadline, beta=500, eta=0.3, strength=0.25, block_inner=2, c_every=3):
   a=norm; best...
   it=0
   while it<1000000 and time<deadline:
      c=conv...; sc...
      update
      # one scaling block
      L,w=_lse_weights(c,beta)
      old=a.copy(); old_sc=sc
      for sub in range(block_inner):
         h=cross...
         log...
         a=norm(...)
      sc_new=score(a)
      if sc_new<old_sc:
         if sc_new<best...
         eta=min(0.8,eta*1.02)
      else:
         a=old; eta=max(0.05,eta*0.8)
      # occasionally c projection
      if c_every>0 and it % c_every == c_every-1:
         Mcur=score/(2n)
         cap target as above
         c_target=...
         a_proj=_c_projection_step(a,c_target,M,n,strength)
         a_proj=norm
         blend=0.5
         a_new=0.5*a+0.5*a_proj
         sc_c=score(a_new)
         if sc_c < score(a):
            a=a_new; update; strength=min(0.7,strength*1.05)
         else: strength=max(0.1,strength*0.92)
      # beta adapt
      if it % 20 ==0: beta=min(200000,beta*1.25)
      it+=1
   return best,best_sc
```
Need avoid recomputing score(a) after c; use last_sc. Keep variable.

This may be time-consuming but okay.

Stochastic restarts:
```
def _perturb(a, rng, kind): ... top-level
def stochastic_search(a, M,n, target_score, deadline, min_slice=30):
   best=a; best_sc=score
   attempts=0
   while time<deadline and attempts<50:
      now=time; remaining=deadline-now
      if remaining < min_slice*2: break
      kind = int(rng.integers(0,7)) if kind? 
      a_p=_perturb(best,rng,kind)
      # allocate slice = min(120, remaining*0.2)
      slice_deadline = now + min(120, max(20, remaining*0.2))
      # choose optimizer: alternating quick or scaling
      if attempts % 3 == 0:
          a_opt, sc_opt = run_alternating(a_p, M,n,target_score, slice_deadline, beta=1000, eta=0.35, strength=0.3, c_every=0)
      elif attempts %3==1:
          a_opt, sc_opt = run_scaling(a_p, M,n, eta=0.35, max_outer=100000, inner=2, beta=2000, deadline=slice_deadline)
      else:
          a_opt, sc_opt = run_cspace(a_p, M,n,target_score, max_iters=10000, deadline=slice_deadline)
      if sc_opt<best_sc: update
      attempts++
   return best,best_sc
```
But `run_alternating` with c_every=0? Need handle if c_every=0 no c. Implement if `c_every >0`.

```
def _pool_add(pool, a, sc):
    pool.append((sc, a.copy()))
    if len(pool) > 10:
        # find max index
        max_idx=0
        for i in range(1,len(pool)):
           if pool[i][0] > pool[max_idx][0]: max_idx=i
```

`propose_candidate` flow code:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    total=float(budget_s)
    deadline=t_start+max(60, total-180)
    parent=None
    try:
       p=globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
       if p is not None:
          p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
          if p.size>=16:
             parent=_normalize(p)
    except: parent=None
    pool=[]
    best_a=None; best_sc=inf

    # candidates
    if parent is not None:
        cand=parent
        if len(cand)<128:
            # keep original and upsample
        elif len(cand)>MAX_N_OPT:
            cand=_resample(cand, min(MAX_N_OPT, 8192))
    else:
        cand=np.ones(4096)
    # evaluate base
    sc=exact_score(cand); update
    # length variations
    lengths=set()
    base_len=len(cand)
    for L in [base_len, int(base_len*0.75), int(base_len*1.25), 1024,2048,4096,8192]:
       if 256 <= L <= MAX_N_OPT: lengths.add(int(L))
    for L in sorted(lengths):
       if L == base_len: continue
       ar=_resample(cand,L); sc=exact_score(ar); update
    # shapes at chosen length (use current best length)
    n0=len(best_a); M0=_next_power2(2*n0-1)
    for sh in generate_shapes(n0, rng):
       sc=exact_score(sh); if sc<best_sc update; pool add
    # Phase 1 alternating
    ph1 = t_start + 0.45*(deadline-t_start)
    a1,sc1=run_alternating(best_a, M0, n0, target_score, ph1, beta=500, eta=0.3, strength=0.25, c_every=4)
    update
    # Phase 2 scaling
    ph2 = t_start + 0.60*(deadline-t_start)
    a2,sc2=run_scaling(best_a, M0,n0, eta=0.35, beta=2000, inner=2, top_every=5, top_k=20, deadline=ph2)
    update
    # Phase 3 L-BFGS
    ph3 = t_start + 0.80*(deadline-t_start)
    betas=[1000,5000,20000,80000]
    for beta in betas:
       if time.monotonic() > ph3: break
       remaining=ph3-now-5
       if remaining < 10: break
       maxiter=int(min(4000, max(200, remaining*1.5)))
       maxfun=int(maxiter*30)
       a3,sc3=run_lbfgs(best_a,beta,maxiter,maxfun,M0,n0)
       update
       # quick scaling polish
       a4,sc4=run_scaling(best_a,M0,n0,eta=0.2,beta=beta*2,inner=2,max_outer=10000,deadline=min(ph3, now+20))
       update
    # Phase 4 cspace
    ph4 = t_start + 0.90*(deadline-t_start)
    target_for_c = min(TARGET_SCORE - 0.001, best_sc - 1e-4)
    if target_for_c < 1.2: target_for_c = best_sc*0.999
    a5,sc5=run_cspace(best_a,M0,n0,target_for_c,max_iters=100000,strength0=0.25,strength_max=0.6,deadline=ph4)
    update
    # Phase 5 stochastic
    a6,sc6=stochastic_search(best_a,M0,n0,min(TARGET_SCORE-0.001,best_sc-1e-4), deadline-30)
    update
    # Final polish
    a7,sc7=run_alternating(best_a,M0,n0,target_for_c, deadline-20, beta=5000, eta=0.2, strength=0.2, c_every=2)
    update
    # Maybe final L-BFGS if time >30
    if time.monotonic() < deadline-30:
       maxiter=int(min(3000, max(100, (deadline-20-now)*1.0)))
       a8,sc8=run_lbfgs(best_a,100000,maxiter,maxiter*30,M0,n0)
       update
    # resample final? Maybe if length > MAX? no.
    # final select from pool
    # ensure best_a finite
    out = _make_output(best_a)
    verified = evaluate_sequence(out)
    # if pool has other, verify top few if time
    ...
    return out
```

Need update best and pool without nested function. We'll manually assign.

When length changes, M0/n0 fixed to best_a length. If best_a changes length? Our optimizers keep length. Resampling lengths initial only. If a candidate from length exploration shorter/longer becomes best, we should set n0/M0 accordingly before phases. We choose best among initial and shapes, then set n0. Need `_resample` with new_n same as old return copy.
```
def _resample(a, new_n):
    a=np.asarray(a)
    old_n=a.size
    if new_n == old_n: return a.copy()
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,old_n)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,new_n)
    b=np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    return _normalize(b)
```

`_gaussian_smooth` maybe for perturb:
```
def _smooth(a, sigma):
    if a.size <5: return a
    r=int(max(1, 3*sigma))
    x=np.arange(-r,r+1)
    ker=np.exp(-0.5*(x/sigma)**2); ker/=ker.sum()
    return np.convolve(a, ker, mode='same')
```

Perturb top-level:
```
def _perturb(a, rng, kind):
    a=a.copy().astype(float)
    n=a.size
    if n <=1: return a
    kind=int(kind) % 7
    if kind==0:
       a *= np.exp(0.03*rng.standard_normal(n))
    elif kind==1:
       a *= np.exp(0.06*rng.standard_normal(n))
       a=_smooth(a, max(1,n//300))
    elif kind==2:
       x=np.arange(n)/max(1,n-1)
       freq=int(rng.integers(1,10))
       phase=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
       a *= 1+0.08*np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*x+phase)
    elif kind==3:
       bs=max(1,n//40)
       for _ in range(6):
           s=int(rng.integers(0,n-bs))
           a[s:s+bs] *= np.exp(float(rng.standard_normal())*0.12)
    elif kind==4:
       mask = rng.random(n) < 0.03
       a[mask]=0.0
    elif kind==5:
       a=_smooth(a, max(1,int(rng.integers(1,n//100+1))))
    else:
       a += 0.005*rng.random(n)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: a=np.ones(n)/n
    else: a=a/s
    return a
```
Avoid nested for loop? top-level helper can have loops. No lambda.

```
def _generate_shapes(n, rng):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n-1) if n>1 else np.zeros(1)
    # Actually use j=np.arange(n)
    cand=[]
    cand.append(np.ones(n))
    j=np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    cand.append(j+1)
    cand.append((max(0,n-1)-j)+1)
    tent=np.minimum(j+1, n-j); cand.append(tent)
    for alpha in (0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0):
       cand.append((j+1)**alpha)
       cand.append((n-j)**alpha)
    try t/(tmax)
    cand.append(1+0.5*np.cos(2*np.pi*j/(n-1+1e-12)))
    cand.append(1+0.8*np.cos(np.pi*j/(n-1+1e-12))) # might negative? min endpoint 0.2? 1-0.8=0.2 OK
    cand.append(np.sin(np.pi*j/(n-1+1e-12)))
    center=j[(n-1)/2.0]; sigma=max(1,n/8); cand.append(np.exp(-(center-center)**2/(2*sigma**2)))
    # random positive fixed
    cand.append(np.maximum(rng.random(n),1e-3))
    out=[]
    seen=set()
    for c in cand:
       a=_normalize(c)
       if a.size==n and a.sum()>0:
          out.append(a)
    return out
```

```
def _make_output(a):
    a=_normalize(a)
    out=[float(max(0.0,min(1000.0,x))) for x in a]
    if not out or sum(out) <=0.01:
       out=[0.5]*64
    return out
```

```
def _verify_candidates(pool, current_out, current_verified):
    if current_verified < inf: return current_out, current_verified
    # verify up to 3 candidates sorted by internal score manually
    cands=[]
    for sc,a in pool:
       cands.append((sc, len(a), a))
    # sort by sc using insertion? small list
    for i in range(len(cands)):
      for j in range(i+1,len(cands)):
        if cands[j][0] < cands[i][0]: swap
    verified_best=current_verified; best_out=current_out
    for sc, low,a in cands[:3]:
       out=_make_output(a)
       try: v=float(evaluate_sequence(out))
       except: v=np.inf
       if np.isfinite(v) and v<verified_best:
           verified_best=v; best_out=out
    return best_out, verified_best
```

```
def run_alternating(a, M, n, target_score, deadline, beta=500.0, eta=0.30, strength=0.25, inner=2, c_every=4, target_margin=1.03):
    a=_normalize(a); if zero return
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score(a)
    beta_cur=float(beta); eta_cur=float(eta); strength_cur=float(strength)
    it=0
    while it < 10000000:
       if deadline is not None and time.monotonic()>deadline: break
       c=_convolution_fft(a,M); Mcur=float(c.max()); sc=2.0*n*Mcur
       if sc<best_sc: update
       # scaling
       L,w=_lse_weights(c,beta_cur)
       old=a.copy(); old_sc=sc
       for sub in range(inner):
          h=_cross_corr(a,w,M); ... a=...
       sc_new=exact_score(a)
       if finite and sc_new<old_sc:
          if sc_new<best_sc update
          eta_cur=min(0.8, eta_cur*1.02)
       else:
          a=old; sc=sc_new if finite else old_sc? set sc=old_sc
          eta_cur=max(0.05, eta_cur*0.8)
       # c projection
       if c_every>0 and it % c_every == c_every-1:
          if Mcur <= 1e-15: pass
          else:
             target_M=target_score/(2.0*n)
             if Mcur <= target_M*1.02: cap=Mcur*0.99
             else:
                cap=math.sqrt(Mcur * (target_M*target_margin))
                if cap <=0 or not finite: cap=Mcur*0.95
                if cap >= Mcur: cap=Mcur*0.95
             ct=_make_target_c(c, cap, smooth=True)
             ap=_c_projection_step(a,ct,M,n,strength_cur)
             ap=_normalize(ap)
             # if ap invalid, use a
             if ap.sum()>0:
                a_new=0.5*a+0.5*ap
                a_new=_normalize(a_new)
                sc_c=exact_score(a_new)
                if finite and sc_c < sc:
                   a=a_new; if sc_c<best_sc update; sc=sc_c
                   strength_cur=min(0.7,strength_cur*1.05)
                else:
                   strength_cur=max(0.1,strength_cur*0.92)
       # beta adaptation
       if it % 20 ==0:
          beta_cur=min(200000.0, beta_cur*1.25)
       it +=1
    if sc<best_sc? update final? Already.
    return best,best_sc
```

Let's write `exact_score` with optional M:
```
def exact_score(a, M=None):
   a=_normalize(a)
   if a.sum()<=0: return inf
   n=a.size
   if M is None: M=_next_power2(2*n-1)
   c=_convolution_fft(a,M)
   return 2*n*max
```
Use in loops? Could pass M but helper exact_score called without M in many. Could keep simple. Not critical.

Now, ensure `stochastic_search` if `target_score` not finite set 1.5.
